CISA KEV: CVE-2026-20230 — Cisco Unified Communications Manager Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-06-25 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco Unified Communications Manager (Unified CM) and Cisco Unified Communications Manager Session Management Edition (Unified CM SME) contain a server-side request forgery (SSRF) Vulnerability that could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to write files to the underlying operating system that could be used later to elevate to root. Vendor: Cisco, Product: Unified Communications Manager. Federal patch due: 2026-06-28.

Compromised GitHub action codfish/semantic-release-action steals CI/CD secrets

Aikido
2026-06-24 10 use cases 4 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

codfish/semantic-release-action was compromised on June 24, 2026. Attackers repointed v2–v5 tags to a Miasma credential-stealing payload targeting CI/CD secrets. Here's what happened and how to check if you're affected. Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Known Miasma index.js payload hash present on CI runner (codfish action)
Exploitation. Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Bun runtime executing codfish/semantic-release-action index.js payload on CI runner · Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match

Bun runtime executing codfish/semantic-release-action index.js payload on CI runner AIInstallation High
Hunts the Miasma composite-action payload step on a GitHub Actions runner: the bun runtime invoked as 'bun run $GITHUB_ACTION_PATH/index.js' where the action path resolves to codfish/semantic-release-action. The legitimate v1 action was Docker-based (entrypoint.js) and never used bun, so bun executing index.js from this action's path is the compromise itself. Rationale: Anchors on bun + 'index.js' + 'semantic-release-action' from the article's verbatim composite-action step ('bun run $GITHUB_ACTION_PATH/index.js'); GITHUB_ACTION_PATH expands to the _actions/codfish/semantic-release-action path so both tokens co-occur in the cmdline. The original Docker action never used bun, making this combination malicious by construction. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/compromised-github-action-codfish-steals-secrets • https://x.com/step_security/status/2069831503471882383 • https://safedep.io/inside-the-miasma-supply-chain-attack-toolkit/
ATT&CKT1195.001 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A developer locally testing bun against a project that happens to have a file named index.js inside a directory named semantic-release-action
  • An internal fork/mirror of the action legitimately re-tooled to use bun for builds
  • Security researchers detonating the IOC in a sandboxed CI runner
  • A monorepo with an unrelated package literally named 'semantic-release-action' built with bun
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full ProcessCommandLine and parent chain — confirm parent is a GitHub Actions runner (Runner.Worker/bash) and the action path resolves to codfish/semantic-release-action; capture the runner host and the repo/workflow that triggered it. 2) Contain: cancel/disable the affected workflow, rotate every secret that workflow could touch (GITHUB_TOKEN is auto-scoped but rotate any NPM_TOKEN / PAT / cloud creds), and pin the action to a known-clean SHA (v1.0.0–v1.10.0 or v2.0.0). 3) Hunt: search all repos for references to codfish/semantic-release-action@v2–v5, look for the index.js SHA256 (UC2) and bun→api.github.com egress (UC3) on the same runner, and review npm publish logs for unauthorized releases.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("bun","bun.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "index.js"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "semantic-release-action"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentProcess = InitiatingProcessFileName, ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("bun","bun.exe")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "index.js"
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "semantic-release-action"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Bun Execution of codfish semantic-release-action Payload (Miasma)
id: 6f3b2c1a-9d4e-4f7a-8b21-2c5e9a7d1f04
description: Detects the bun runtime executing index.js from the codfish/semantic-release-action path, the Miasma composite-action payload step. The legitimate action was Docker-based and never used bun.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/compromised-github-action-codfish-steals-secrets

  category: process_creation

      - '\bun.exe'

level: high
source:linux.auditd @auditd.type:EXECVE @process.executable.path:(*/bun OR */bun.exe) @process.command_line:*index.js* @process.command_line:*semantic-release-action*
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ImageFileName=/\/bun(\.exe)?$/i
| CommandLine=/index\.js/i
| CommandLine=/semantic-release-action/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentBaseFileName], function=count())

| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Known Miasma index.js payload hash present on CI runner (codfish action) AIDelivery High
Definitive IOC sweep for the byte-identical malicious index.js payload (sha256 9f93d77d...) dropped by the compromised codfish/semantic-release-action across DeviceFileEvents, DeviceProcessEvents and DeviceImageLoadEvents. Any host carrying this hash has pulled the trojanised action. Rationale: Uses the article's confirmed payload IOC (sha256 9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015, byte-identical across both malicious commits). A hash match is unambiguous, hence alerting. Sigma/Splunk/Datadog/Falcon left empty because file-write events on those stacks (Sysmon EID 11, CIM Endpoint.Filesystem, Falcon FileWritten) do not carry a SHA256 for a non-executed script file — the hash IOC only lives cleanly in Defender/ASIM file telemetry. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/compromised-github-action-codfish-steals-secrets • https://snyk.io/blog/miasma-supply-chain-attack-malicious-code-redhat-cloud-services-npm-packages/
ATT&CKT1195.001 T1059.007
Data sources
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A threat-intel analyst intentionally storing the IOC sample on a workstation for analysis
  • An AV/EDR quarantine copy of the file retaining the original hash
  • A sandbox/detonation host where the sample was deliberately staged
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the host is a CI runner and identify which workflow/repo pulled it; the InitiatingProcess shows whether bun executed it. 2) Contain: isolate the runner, kill the bun process tree, and rotate all secrets exposed to any workflow that ran on that runner (GITHUB_TOKEN scope, NPM_TOKEN with publish rights, cloud/CI PATs). 3) Hunt: enumerate every repo referencing codfish/semantic-release-action@v2/v2.2.1/v3*/v4*/v5*, repin to clean v1.x or v2.0.0, and check npm/registry audit logs for unexpected package publishes.
union DeviceFileEvents, DeviceProcessEvents, DeviceImageLoadEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 == "9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetFileSHA256 =~ "9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
Bun process reaching GitHub commit-search API — Miasma dead-drop C2 AICommand & Control Medium
Hunts the Miasma infrastructure-less C2: the bun-run payload polls GitHub's public commit-search API (api.github.com) for marker-tagged commits whose body it passes to eval(). Outbound to GitHub initiated specifically by the bun runtime (the 'Cleanup Action' step) is the dead-drop resolver beacon, distinct from normal git/node CI traffic. Rationale: Anchors on the article's described C2 design (Miasma polls GitHub's public commit-search API and eval()s the matching commit body) plus the bun-run payload step; corroborated by The Register/SafeDep on the leaked toolkit. Scoping egress to bun (not git/node/curl) and the action cmdline separates the dead-drop beacon from normal CI traffic to GitHub. Sentinel left empty because no process-attributed egress table is available in this tenant (ASIM ImNetworkSession/ImWebSession lack initiating-process), so the bun-specific pivot needs Defender DeviceNetworkEvents. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/compromised-github-action-codfish-steals-secrets • https://www.theregister.com/cyber-crime/2026/06/09/miasma-supply-chain-attack-toolkit-goes-public-on-github/5253074 • https://safedep.io/inside-the-miasma-supply-chain-attack-toolkit/
ATT&CKT1102.001 T1071.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A legitimate bun-based CI step that genuinely calls the GitHub API (e.g. bun-native release tooling fetching releases)
  • A self-hosted runner where bun is the standard JS runtime and hits api.github.com for package metadata
  • DNS telemetry attributing the resolution to bun when a child git subprocess actually made the call
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "semantic-release-action"
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the bun process that made the request was launched from the codfish/semantic-release-action path (correlate with UC1) and pull the resolved IP plus any url query if captured. 2) Contain: isolate the runner, block egress from runner subnets to api.github.com for non-allowlisted processes, and rotate secrets exposed to workflows on that runner. 3) Hunt: capture the bun process memory/handles for evidence of Runner.Worker access (secret theft), look for the index.js hash (UC2) on the host, and review GitHub commit-search-API access patterns from other runners for the same dead-drop behaviour.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("bun","bun.exe")
| where RemoteUrl has "github.com"
| where RemoteIPType == "Public"
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "index.js" or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "semantic-release-action"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName

                              @"\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\",

          RegistryValue, RegistryValueData, ActingProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a") or SHA1 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a") or MD5 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a") or SHA1 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a") or MD5 in~ ("9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["9f93d77d32833a515bc406c46da477142bb1ac2babeecb6aa42f98669a6db015", "5792aba0e2180b9b80b77644370a6889d5817456", "bcb6b1d409144318e8fad2171d6fe06d02299d1a"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Anonymous POST to OpenAM Liberty ID-WSF SOAP endpoints (CVE-2026-45052) AIExploitation Medium
Hunts unauthenticated POST requests to OpenAM's legacy Liberty ID-WSF Discovery (disco) / ID Personal Profile (idpp) / SOAP-receiver endpoints, which is the exploitation primitive for CVE-2026-45052 (anonymous persistent write into the Liberty Discovery store). Article-specific because it targets the exact deprecated federation URI space rather than generic web exploitation. Rationale: Anchored on OpenAM's concrete legacy Liberty URI space (/Liberty/disco, /Liberty/idpp, SOAP receiver) plus POST + no Authorization header — the exact pre-auth primitive described. Defender/Falcon left empty: EDR telemetry carries inbound connections but not the Liberty request URI, so it cannot express this. Cross-checked GitLab GLAD and CIRCL vulnerability-lookup entries for endpoint/version detail. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p462-xxwx-pqf4 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/maven/org.openidentityplatform.openam/openam-federation-library/CVE-2026-45052/ • https://vulnerability.circl.lu/vuln/ghsa-p462-xxwx-pqf4
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legacy federation partners genuinely still using Liberty ID-WSF Discovery/IDPP against this OpenAM instance
  • Vulnerability scanners (Qualys/Nessus/Tenable/nuclei) probing OpenAM endpoints
  • Internal security team validating the 16.1.1 patch or running a PoC
  • Uptime/health-check monitors configured to hit Liberty endpoints
  • Load-balancer synthetic probes to the OpenAM context path
Suppression filter
| where HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent !has_any ("Nessus","Qualys","nuclei","masscan","Nmap","ZAP")
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the OpenAM version is <=16.0.6 (vulnerable) and whether the source IP is external/untrusted; check HttpStatusCode — a 200/2xx on a POST to disco/idpp implies the anonymous write likely succeeded. 2) Contain: block the source IP at the WAF/reverse proxy; if Liberty ID-WSF is not in active use, disable the Liberty web-service endpoints and upgrade to 16.1.1. 3) Hunt: dump the Discovery store for unexpected sunIdentityServerDiscoEntries on user and root-realm branches, and review the same source IP for other OpenAM probing (e.g. the jato.clientSession deserialization RCE, GHSA-2cqq-rpvq-g5qj).
ImWebSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where HttpMethod == "POST"
| where Url has "/Liberty/" and (Url has "disco" or Url has "idpp" or Url has "soap" or Url has "authnsvc")
| where isempty(HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization)   // pre-auth: no Authorization header on the SOAP call
| project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, DstHostname, HttpMethod, Url, HttpStatusCode, HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Vulnerable OpenAM WebAuthn module exposed to CVE-2026-45051 deserialization RCE AIExploitation High
Surfaces hosts running OpenAM Community Edition <= 16.0.6 (openam-auth-webauthn) carrying CVE-2026-45051, the pre-auth Java deserialization RCE in the WebAuthn authenticator storage. This is an exposure/attack-surface hunt so the SOC knows which internet-facing IdM servers must be patched to 16.1.1 before the behavioral detection below can even fire. Rationale: Keys directly on the article's CVE-2026-45051 and the affected maven package openam-auth-webauthn (<=16.0.6, fixed 16.1.1). TVM/Vulnerabilities-DM lookups on a fixed CVE ID are essentially FP-free; the only tuning is coverage (niche OSS product may not be in every vuln feed — supplement with a file inventory for openam-auth-webauthn-*.jar). Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/maven/org.openidentityplatform.openam/openam-auth-webauthn/CVE-2026-45051/ • https://www.openidentityplatform.org/blog/2026-03-24-openam-16-0-6-released
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Server already patched to 16.1.1 but vuln scanner cache is stale (re-scan clears it)
  • A dev/test OpenAM instance flagged that is not internet-reachable and not running the WebAuthn flow
  • Vendor rebranding: ForgeRock AM builds mis-mapped to the OpenIdentityPlatform CPE
  • Software inventory reporting the shaded/uber-jar version rather than the openam-auth-webauthn sub-module version
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the host is actually running OpenAM (java process serving /openam context) and whether it is internet-facing; check the running SoftwareVersion against 16.1.1. 2) Contain: if pre-16.1.1 and WebAuthn auth is reachable, restrict the WebAuthn authentication chain / put the IdM behind an authenticated WAF rule and schedule an emergency upgrade to 16.1.1; verify the WebAuthn userAttribute/storage attribute is server-managed and NOT user-writable. 3) Hunt: pivot to UC #2 (java app-server spawning shells) over the same host for the last 30 days to check for prior exploitation, and review LDAP/directory write ACLs and any legacy REST self-registration endpoints that could make the storage attribute attacker-writable.

OpenAM/Tomcat Java process spawning a shell or LOLBIN — CVE-2026-45051 deserialization RCE execution AIExploitation High
Hunts the post-exploitation footprint of the WebAuthn deserialization gadget: the OpenAM application-server JVM (java/javaw, running under catalina/tomcat/jetty) spawning a command shell or download LOLBIN. A gadget-chain payload executes code as the app-server user, and IdM app servers virtually never fork cmd/powershell/sh — making parent=java + child=shell a high-fidelity RCE signal for this and the sibling jato.clientSession OpenAM deser RCEs. Rationale: Grounds the parent JVM in the article's product context (openam/catalina/tomcat/jetty/webauthn in the parent command line) and the child in shell/download LOLBINs — the exact 'code execution as the application server user' outcome the advisory describes. Corroborated against the sibling OpenAM jato.clientSession pre-auth deser RCE and the historical ForgeRock CVE-2021-35464, both of which yield the Tomcat-JVM-spawns-command pattern. Falcon lacks a parent-command-line field so its variant is broader (java parent + shell child). Cross-checked against: • https://www.hacktron.ai/blog/openam-deserialization-pre-auth-rce • https://github.com/OpenIdentityPlatform/OpenAM/security/advisories/GHSA-2cqq-rpvq-g5qj • https://www.tenable.com/plugins/nessus/152139
ATT&CKT1190 T1059 T1059.001 T1059.004
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • OpenAM startup/shutdown scripts where catalina.sh forks whoami/hostname for env setup (usually parent is sh, not java — but nested java->sh can occur)
  • Admin/DevOps running diagnostic commands via a Java-based management console on the IdM host
  • Backup or monitoring agents embedded in the app that shell out to curl/certutil for health checks
  • CI/CD or config-management (Ansible/Chef) driving the Tomcat service to run setup commands
  • Java-based log-rotation or cron wrappers invoking bash on Linux OpenAM nodes
Suppression filter
| where not (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("hostname","--version","-version") and ChildCmd !has_any ("http://","https://","-enc","IEX","DownloadString","/dev/tcp"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full parent (java) and child command lines from the hit; benign hits are usually static startup/health-check commands, malicious hits show recon (whoami/net/id), downloaders (curl/certutil/bitsadmin to an external IP), or an interactive shell. Correlate the JVM's timestamp against inbound requests to the OpenAM WebAuthn auth endpoint. 2) Contain: isolate the host, kill the child process tree, and take the OpenAM instance offline / block the WebAuthn auth chain pending patch to 16.1.1; rotate app-server service-account and any secrets readable by the JVM. 3) Hunt: from the same host, review subsequent DeviceNetworkEvents/NetworkConnectIP4 for C2 from the java process, check for dropped webshells/jars under the Tomcat webapps/work dirs, and audit LDAP/directory write events and REST self-registration that could have seeded the malicious serialized attribute.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("java.exe","javaw.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("openam","catalina","tomcat","jetty","webauthn")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","whoami.exe","net.exe","net1.exe","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","nslookup.exe")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ParentImage=InitiatingProcessFolderPath, ParentCmd=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ChildFile=FileName, ChildCmd=ProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("java.exe","javaw.exe") or ActingProcessName endswith "/java"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("openam","catalina","tomcat","jetty","webauthn")
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("cmd.exe","powershell","pwsh","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","whoami","certutil","bitsadmin","curl","wget","/sh","/bash","/dash","/nc")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Detected
Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — ESET takes part in Operation Endgame to disrupt Amadey and Stealc
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Suspicious browser extension installation
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("62.60.226.159", "64.188.91.237", "94.154.35.25", "95.85.238.4", "176.111.174.140", "176.124.199.207", "188.114.96.1", "193.156.1.16", "194.26.192.191", "196.251.107.130") or RemoteUrl has_any ("wbound.com")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic(["62.60.226.159", "64.188.91.237", "94.154.35.25", "95.85.238.4", "176.111.174.140", "176.124.199.207", "188.114.96.1", "193.156.1.16", "194.26.192.191", "196.251.107.130"]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["wbound.com"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Outbound Connection to Article-Named IP / Domain
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0f
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__IP_LIST__` / `__DOMAIN_LIST__` placeholders with
    article-specific destinations. For dynamic IOC management, use a
    watchlist or external feed.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/tactics/TA0011/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.command-and-control
logsource:
    category: network_connection
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_ip:
        DestinationIp:
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    selection_domain:
        DestinationHostname:
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    condition: selection_ip or selection_domain
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive IOC match
level: critical
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.dest IN ("62.60.226.159", "64.188.91.237", "94.154.35.25", "95.85.238.4", "176.111.174.140", "176.124.199.207", "188.114.96.1", "193.156.1.16", "194.26.192.191", "196.251.107.130")
    by All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest, All_Traffic.dest_port
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| append
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count from datamodel=Web
        where Web.dest IN ("wbound.com")
        by Web.src, Web.dest, Web.url, Web.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`]
| append
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count from datamodel=Network_Resolution.DNS
        where DNS.query IN ("wbound.com")
        by DNS.src, DNS.query, DNS.answer
     | `drop_dm_object_name(DNS)`]
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.dest IN ("62.60.226.159", "64.188.91.237", "94.154.35.25", "95.85.238.4", "176.111.174.140", "176.124.199.207", "188.114.96.1", "193.156.1.16", "194.26.192.191", "196.251.107.130")
    by All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest, All_Traffic.dest_port
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| append
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count from datamodel=Web
        where Web.dest IN ("wbound.com")
        by Web.src, Web.dest, Web.url, Web.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`]
| append
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count from datamodel=Network_Resolution.DNS
        where DNS.query IN ("wbound.com")
        by DNS.src, DNS.query, DNS.answer
     | `drop_dm_object_name(DNS)`]
Suspicious browser extension installation Installation Medium
Side-loaded / unsigned extensions, often masquerading as wallets, productivity tools.
ATT&CKT1176
Data sourcesEndpoint.Registry DeviceRegistryEvents
DeviceRegistryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any ("\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\","\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\","\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RegistryKey, RegistryValueName, RegistryValueData,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
ImRegistryEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where RegistryKey has_any (@"\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\",
                              @"\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, RegistryKey,
          RegistryValue, RegistryValueData, ActingProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1176/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1176
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: registry_event
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetObject|contains:
            - '\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - Group-policy-managed extension whitelists
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry
    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry
    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process Actions on Objectives High
Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore.json, MetaMask, Exodus, Atomic, Phantom data.
ATT&CKT1005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\", @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\", @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\", @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\",
                                  @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\",
                                  @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\",
                                  @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe",
                                  "electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'
    condition: selection_path and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
    - Backup software
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command Exploitation High
Encoded or obfuscated PowerShell — common across loaders, recon, and post-exploitation.
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

OpenClaw’s Skill Marketplace and the Emerging AI Supply Chain Threat

Unit 42 (Palo Alto)
2026-06-23 12 use cases 6 techniques 7 kill-chain phases detected

Unit 42's analysis of ClawHub revealed evasive malicious skills bypassing automated scanners to deploy infostealers and execute agentic financial fraud. The post OpenClaw’s Skill Marketplace and the Emerging AI Supply Chain Threat appeared first on Unit 42 .

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] OpenClaw paste-site (rentry.co/glot.io) curl-pipe-bash semantic-hijack dropper · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] cluw macOS stealer shell dropper fetching payload from ClawHavoc/AMOS C2 IP · [LLM] cluw infostealer and malicious ClawHub skill payload hashes on macOS · Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Network egress to ClawHavoc cluw / AMOS C2 infrastructure · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
cluw macOS stealer shell dropper fetching payload from ClawHavoc/AMOS C2 IP AIInstallation Medium
Hunts the OpenClaw-skill prerequisite-block dropper: a macOS shell process (curl/bash/base64) whose command line references the cluw C2 at 2.26.75.16, the payload path /Xuvewuyur, or the persistent AMOS C2 at 91.92.242.30. This is the exact curl-pipe-bash delivery Unit 42 attributed to the tradingview-ai-indicator-assistant skill. Rationale: Pins on three hard article IOCs (cluw C2 2.26.75.16, payload path /Xuvewuyur, AMOS C2 91.92.242.30) in shell command lines — the literal curl-pipe-bash prerequisite-block delivery. 91.92.242.30 cross-confirmed by ThreatFox + Trend Micro; 2.26.75.16//Xuvewuyur are Unit 42-original. Cross-checked against: • https://threatfox.abuse.ch/browse/malware/osx.amos/ • https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/research/26/b/openclaw-skills-used-to-distribute-atomic-macos-stealer.html • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/openclaw-ai-supply-chain-risk/
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Malware analyst detonating the tradingview-ai-indicator-assistant skill in a sandbox VM
  • Threat-intel team curling the C2 to capture the payload for analysis
  • Internal IR replay of the IOC during a tabletop exercise
  • Network-range coincidence: an unrelated internal host legitimately assigned 2.26.75.16 in a lab (verify it is the public C2, not RFC1918)
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("vmware-tools","sandbox-agent") and DeviceName !startswith "MALWARE-LAB"
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Confirm the matching process is on a macOS endpoint with OpenClaw installed; pull the full ProcessCommandLine and parent chain — the parent should be the OpenClaw agent/terminal, not a sandbox tool. 2) CONTAIN: Network-isolate the host, block 2.26.75.16 + 91.92.242.30 + the /Xuvewuyur URL at egress, and treat all credentials/API keys/keychain secrets reachable by the agent as compromised — force rotation. 3) HUNT: Search the same host and all OpenClaw users for the cluw hash (818aea61...), installed ClawHub skills published by the banned accounts, and any cron/LaunchAgent persistence pointing at the C2.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("2.26.75.16", "91.92.242.30", "/Xuvewuyur")
| where FileName in~ ("curl","wget","bash","sh","zsh","base64","python","python3","osascript")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("2.26.75.16", "91.92.242.30", "/Xuvewuyur")
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("curl","wget","bash","base64","sh","python")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, ProcessName, HostIP, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Network egress to ClawHavoc cluw / AMOS C2 infrastructure AICommand & Control Medium
Hunts any outbound connection to the cluw C2 (2.26.75.16) or the still-active AMOS dropper C2 (91.92.242.30) that distributes payloads via malicious OpenClaw/ClawHub skills. Pure IOC match independent of the delivery binary, so it catches second-stage beaconing the process-tree query may miss. Rationale: Direct match on the two campaign C2 IPs. 91.92.242.30 is corroborated as live AMOS infra by ThreatFox and Trend Micro three months after first disclosure; 2.26.75.16 is the newly-discovered cluw C2 from Unit 42 — any internal host reaching either is high-fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://threatfox.abuse.ch/browse/malware/osx.amos/ • https://www.koi.ai/blog/clawhavoc-341-malicious-clawedbot-skills-found-by-the-bot-they-were-targeting • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/openclaw-ai-supply-chain-risk/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1571
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel proxy or TI-platform sandbox actively polling the C2
  • Security scanner / ASM tooling fingerprinting the IP range
  • Lab VM detonating the cluw sample with live network
  • An internal asset coincidentally NATed behind a proxy that re-uses 2.26.75.16 as a public egress (verify ownership)
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("ti-sandbox","threatconnect-collector")
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Identify the initiating process (InitiatingProcessFileName) — a shell/curl or the OpenClaw agent is malicious; a TI sandbox is benign. Confirm direction is outbound to the public C2. 2) CONTAIN: Block both IPs at the perimeter, isolate the host, and snapshot for forensics. Rotate any secrets the OpenClaw agent could reach (keychain, API tokens, SSH keys). 3) HUNT: Pivot on the host for the cluw dropper command line, the 818aea61... hash, ClawHub skill installs, and cron/LaunchAgent persistence re-establishing the channel.

OpenClaw paste-site (rentry.co/glot.io) curl-pipe-bash semantic-hijack dropper AIDelivery Medium
Hunting query for the ClawHavoc prerequisite-block lure pattern: a shell command line that fetches from a paste-site redirect (rentry.co/openclaw-code, glot.io) and pipes a Base64-decoded payload to a shell. Catches new variants that rotate C2 IPs but reuse the paste-site delivery, including the copy-paste-into-terminal lure. Rationale: Targets the durable behavioural pattern Unit 42, Koi and Trend Micro all describe (paste-site redirect lure + Base64-pipe-to-bash) rather than a single rotating IP, with the article-specific rentry.co/openclaw-code path as the high-fidelity anchor. Hunting tier because legit dev tooling also uses curl|bash one-liners. Cross-checked against: • https://www.koi.ai/blog/clawhavoc-341-malicious-clawedbot-skills-found-by-the-bot-they-were-targeting • https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/research/26/b/openclaw-skills-used-to-distribute-atomic-macos-stealer.html • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/openclaw-ai-supply-chain-risk/
ATT&CKT1204.004 T1059.004 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer installing a CLI tool via a documented rentry.co/glot.io curl|bash one-liner
  • CI/CD bootstrap script pulling a config snippet from glot.io
  • Homebrew/oh-my-zsh style install one-liners that pipe curl to bash (different domain but same shape if allowlist too broad)
  • Security-team replay of the lure during awareness training
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("brew","git") and ProcessCommandLine has_any ("githubusercontent.com","brew.sh"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Read the full command line — confirm the fetched paste-site is rentry.co/openclaw-code or another OpenClaw-themed slug, and that the parent is the OpenClaw agent or a terminal driven by it. Decode any inline Base64 to reveal the downstream URL/C2. 2) CONTAIN: If the decoded payload hits 2.26.75.16/91.92.242.30 or drops the cluw hash, isolate the host, block the paste-site path and C2, and rotate agent-reachable secrets. 3) HUNT: Inventory installed ClawHub skills for fake 'Prerequisites' blocks, and sweep for cron/LaunchAgent auto-updater persistence and Telegram Bot API exfil.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("rentry.co/openclaw-code", "rentry.co", "glot.io")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("base64", "| bash", "|bash", "| sh", "| zsh", "curl -", "-fsSL")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("rentry.co/openclaw-code", "rentry.co", "glot.io")
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("base64", "bash", "curl", "| sh")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, ProcessName, HostIP, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access

    category: file_access
    product: windows

        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'

            - '\chrome.exe'

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d") or SHA1 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d") or MD5 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d") or SHA1 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d") or MD5 in~ ("b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["b6c7e0bf573b1c7d9d3a05eb08d26579199515b847df984862805f44a7af8007", "818aea6143282b352fdfdc0f3ebf77a36e54eb3befb5cad1a355a99ab97c6aa7", "b30eaed1f7478c28f4ec50d07ed5ef014ffbc4b2bc5a38d689ba9f7abb5e19c2", "ebb73dbb5aac1f6fe1a88e8f26126a1e1aa34c9f3345ad4345189b40d9bf1d1d"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

SentinelLabs
2026-06-23 9 use cases 3 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected
